People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. I’d see the proof, but I’d keep thinking maybe there was some delay or message from me that caused harm quietly and just hasn’t been found yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear that you harmed someone could be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know the chart doesn’t show a bad outcome, but it’s hard for me to believe the fear is mistaken. [
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n10\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F42.2, F41.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I keep feeling like I don’t deserve the trust people put in me. I’ll replay little imperfections from work over and over, and it starts to feel like they prove I’m a dangerous doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about being a dangerous doctor feel intrusive and unwanted, as if they pop into your mind against your will?\n\nPatient: They do feel like they just keep coming back, but it’s more than a passing thought. Even if I review the chart and there’s no sign anything went wrong, I still believe some delay or imperfect message from me must have harmed someone somehow. And that eventually everyone will find out.\n\nDoctor: When these thoughts hit, do you find yourself checking charts or seeking reassurance to feel certain no one was harmed?\n\nPatient: I do review the charts, but it never really settles it. Even when there’s nothing showing a bad outcome, I still feel like a routine delay or something I worded imperfectly must have hurt someone without anyone realizing yet—and that eventually people will find out.\n\nDoctor: At the moment you have these thoughts, do you believe for certain that you have harmed someone, or do you recognize that this might be your mind exaggerating the possibility?\n\nPatient: I believe it. Even when the chart shows no bad outcome, I think a delay or something I said the wrong way must have harmed someone somehow, and that eventually everyone will find out.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone you trust been able to convince you, even briefly, that you may not have harmed someone?\n\nPatient: Not really. People can tell me the chart looks fine, but I still think something may have been missed—that a delay or poorly worded message harmed someone, and it’ll come out eventually.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are signs right now that other people already know you harmed someone, even if they have not said it directly?\n\nPatient: Yes. I notice people being brief with me or looking at me a certain way, and I think they may already know something happened but aren’t saying it. Even though I can’t point to anything definite in the chart, it feels like they’ll all find out eventually.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your coworkers are talking about your having harmed someone, even when you cannot hear what they are saying?\n\nPatient: I can’t hear them talking, but I do worry they know. If they’re being brief or acting differently, I think it may be because they’ve figured out that I harmed someone somehow, even though I can’t find anything in the chart.\n\nDoctor: If you were shown clear proof that no patient was harmed, would you still believe you had harmed someone?\n\nPatient: I think I still would. I’d see the proof, but I’d keep thinking maybe there was some delay or message from me that caused harm quietly and just hasn’t been found yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear that you harmed someone could be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know the chart doesn’t show a bad outcome, but it’s hard for me to believe the fear is mistaken. I keep thinking a routine delay or an imperfect message from me may have quietly harmed someone, and eventually people will find out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this belief that you harmed someone comes from your own mind, or does it ever feel like it is being put into your mind by some outside force?\n\nPatient: It feels like it comes from my own mind. I don’t feel like anyone or anything is putting the thought there—it just keeps coming back, and I can’t shake it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

Even if I review the chart and there’s no sign anything went wrong, I still believe some delay or imperfect message from me must have harmed someone somehow. And that eventually everyone will find out.\n\nDoctor: When these thoughts hit, do you find yourself checking charts or seeking reassurance to feel certain no one was harmed?\n\nPatient: I do review the charts, but it never really settles it. Even when there’s nothing showing a bad outcome, I still feel like a routine delay or something I worded imperfectly must have hurt someone without anyone realizing yet—and that eventually people will find out.\n\nDoctor: At the moment you have these thoughts, do you believe for certain that you have harmed someone, or do you recognize that this might be your mind exaggerating the possibility?\n\nPatient: I believe it. Even when the chart shows no bad outcome, I think a delay or something I said the wrong way must have harmed someone somehow, and that eventually everyone will find out.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone you trust been able to convince you, even briefly, that you may not have harmed someone?\n\nPatient: Not really. People can tell me the chart looks fine, but I still think something may have been missed—that a delay or poorly worded message harmed someone, and it’ll come out eventually.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are signs right now that other people already know you harmed someone, even if they have not said it directly?\n\nPatient: Yes. I notice people being brief with me or looking at me a certain way, and I think they may already know something happened but aren’t saying it. Even though I can’t point to anything definite in the chart, it feels like they’ll all find out eventually.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your coworkers are talking about your having harmed someone, even when you cannot hear what they are saying?\n\nPatient: I can’t hear them talking, but I do worry they know. If they’re being brief or acting differently, I think it may be because they’ve figured out that I harmed someone somehow, even though I can’t find anything in the chart.\n\nDoctor: If you were shown clear proof that no patient was harmed, would you still believe you had harmed someone?\n\nPatient: I think I still would. I’d see the proof, but I’d keep thinking maybe there was some delay or message from me that caused harm quietly and just hasn’t been found yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear that you harmed someone could be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know the chart doesn’t show a bad outcome, but it’s hard for me to believe the fear is mistaken. I keep thinking a routine delay or an imperfect message from me may have quietly harmed someone, and eventually people will find out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this belief that you harmed someone comes from your own mind, or does it ever feel like it is being put into your mind by some outside force?\n\nPatient: It feels like it comes from my own mind. I don’t feel like anyone or anything is putting the thought there—it just keeps coming back, and I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: When this fear comes up, do you do anything specific to try to undo it or make sure no harm happened?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I do anything specific to undo it. I mostly just keep going over it in my head, wondering if I missed something.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F42.2, F41.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I keep feeling like I don’t deserve the trust people put in me. I’ll replay little imperfections from work over and over, and it starts to feel like they prove I’m a dangerous doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about being a dangerous doctor feel intrusive and unwanted, as if they pop into your mind against your will?\n\nPatient: They do feel like they just keep coming back, but it’s more than a passing thought. Even if I review the chart and there’s no sign anything went wrong, I still believe some delay or imperfect message from me must have harmed someone somehow. And that eventually everyone will find out.\n\nDoctor: When these thoughts hit, do you find yourself checking charts or seeking reassurance to feel certain no one was harmed?\n\nPatient: I do review the charts, but it never really settles it. Even when there’s nothing showing a bad outcome, I still feel like a routine delay or something I worded imperfectly must have hurt someone without anyone realizing yet—and that eventually people will find out.\n\nDoctor: At the moment you have these thoughts, do you believe for certain that you have harmed someone, or do you recognize that this might be your mind exaggerating the possibility?\n\nPatient: I believe it. Even when the chart shows no bad outcome, I think a delay or something I said the wrong way must have harmed someone somehow, and that eventually everyone will find out.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone you trust been able to convince you, even briefly, that you may not have harmed someone?\n\nPatient: Not really. People can tell me the chart looks fine, but I still think something may have been missed—that a delay or poorly worded message harmed someone, and it’ll come out eventually.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are signs right now that other people already know you harmed someone, even if they have not said it directly?\n\nPatient: Yes. I notice people being brief with me or looking at me a certain way, and I think they may already know something happened but aren’t saying it. Even though I can’t point to anything definite in the chart, it feels like they’ll all find out eventually.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your coworkers are talking about your having harmed someone, even when you cannot hear what they are saying?\n\nPatient: I can’t hear them talking, but I do worry they know. If they’re being brief or acting differently, I think it may be because they’ve figured out that I harmed someone somehow, even though I can’t find anything in the chart.\n\nDoctor: If you were shown clear proof that no patient was harmed, would you still believe you had harmed someone?\n\nPatient: I think I still would. I’d see the proof, but I’d keep thinking maybe there was some delay or message from me that caused harm quietly and just hasn’t been found yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear that you harmed someone could be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know the chart doesn’t show a bad outcome, but it’s hard for me to believe the fear is mistaken. I keep thinking a routine delay or an imperfect message from me may have quietly harmed someone, and eventually people will find out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this belief that you harmed someone comes from your own mind, or does it ever feel like it is being put into your mind by some outside force?\n\nPatient: It feels like it comes from my own mind. I don’t feel like anyone or anything is putting the thought there—it just keeps coming back, and I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: When this fear comes up, do you do anything specific to try to undo it or make sure no harm happened?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I do anything specific to undo it. I mostly just keep going over it in my head, wondering if I missed something.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is actual evidence that you harmed a patient?\n\nPatient: When I review the chart, there isn’t evidence that anything bad happened. But I still feel like a delay or something I said imperfectly may have caused harm that just hasn’t been recognized yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that this belief might be unreasonable or not make sense?\n\nPatient: I know the chart doesn’t show a bad outcome, so part of me can see why it might not make sense. But I still believe maybe a routine delay or something I communicated poorly caused harm that hasn’t been noticed yet. I keep thinking eventually everyone will find out.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Even though I can’t point to anything definite in the chart, it feels like they’ll all find out eventually.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your coworkers are talking about your having harmed someone, even when you cannot hear what they are saying?\n\nPatient: I can’t hear them talking, but I do worry they know. If they’re being brief or acting differently, I think it may be because they’ve figured out that I harmed someone somehow, even though I can’t find anything in the chart.\n\nDoctor: If you were shown clear proof that no patient was harmed, would you still believe you had harmed someone?\n\nPatient: I think I still would. I’d see the proof, but I’d keep thinking maybe there was some delay or message from me that caused harm quietly and just hasn’t been found yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear that you harmed someone could be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know the chart doesn’t show a bad outcome, but it’s hard for me to believe the fear is mistaken. I keep thinking a routine delay or an imperfect message from me may have quietly harmed someone, and eventually people will find out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this belief that you harmed someone comes from your own mind, or does it ever feel like it is being put into your mind by some outside force?\n\nPatient: It feels like it comes from my own mind. I don’t feel like anyone or anything is putting the thought there—it just keeps coming back, and I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: When this fear comes up, do you do anything specific to try to undo it or make sure no harm happened?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I do anything specific to undo it. I mostly just keep going over it in my head, wondering if I missed something.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is actual evidence that you harmed a patient?\n\nPatient: When I review the chart, there isn’t evidence that anything bad happened. But I still feel like a delay or something I said imperfectly may have caused harm that just hasn’t been recognized yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that this belief might be unreasonable or not make sense?\n\nPatient: I know the chart doesn’t show a bad outcome, so part of me can see why it might not make sense. But I still believe maybe a routine delay or something I communicated poorly caused harm that hasn’t been noticed yet. I keep thinking eventually everyone will find out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could be mistaken about your coworkers’ briefness or looks meaning they know you harmed someone?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe I could be, but it doesn’t feel that way. Even with nothing in the chart showing a bad outcome, I keep thinking a routine delay or something I said wrong may have harmed someone, and that eventually everyone will realize it.\n\nDoctor: Outside of this fear about having harmed a patient, do you worry excessively about many other parts of your life?\n\nPatient: Not really. It’s mostly this one fear that keeps coming back.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

{"intent":"Differentiate delusional conviction from an obsessive, ego-dystonic fear by testing whether the patient experiences the thought as senseless/excessive.","question":"When this fear comes up, does it feel senseless or unreasonable to you?"}

It has started to affect how reliably I function at work and how connected I am outside of it.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 1 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about a year and a month, starting after I received the clinic award.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 30 / physician / associates\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S030\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you think you could be mistaken about your coworkers’ briefness or looks meaning they know you harmed someone?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe I could be, but it doesn’t feel that way. Even with nothing in the chart showing a bad outcome, I keep thinking a routine delay or something I said wrong may have harmed someone, and that eventually everyone will realize it.\n\nDoctor: Outside of this fear about having harmed a patient, do you worry excessively about many other parts of your life?\n\nPatient: Not really. It’s mostly this one fear that keeps coming back.\n\nDoctor: When this fear comes up, does it feel senseless or unreasonable to you?\n\nPatient: I can see how it sounds unreasonable when I look at the chart and there’s no evidence anything went wrong. But it doesn’t feel senseless to me. I keep thinking some small delay or something I communicated imperfectly may have quietly harmed a patient, and that eventually people will find out.\n\n[Last Question]\nWhen this fear comes up, do you find yourself mentally reviewing the event over and over to try to be sure no one was harmed?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F42.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I keep feeling like I don’t deserve the trust people put in me. I’ll replay little imperfections from work over and over, and it starts to feel like they prove I’m a dangerous doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about being a dangerous doctor feel intrusive and unwanted, as if they pop into your mind against your will?\n\nPatient: They do feel like they just keep coming back, but it’s more than a passing thought. Even if I review the chart and there’s no sign anything went wrong, I still believe some delay or imperfect message from me must have harmed someone somehow. And that eventually everyone will find out.\n\nDoctor: When these thoughts hit, do you find yourself checking charts or seeking reassurance to feel certain no one was harmed?\n\nPatient: I do review the charts, but it never really settles it. Even when there’s nothing showing a bad outcome, I still feel like a routine delay or something I worded imperfectly must have hurt someone without anyone realizing yet—and that eventually people will find out.\n\nDoctor: At the moment you have these thoughts, do you believe for certain that you have harmed someone, or do you recognize that this might be your mind exaggerating the possibility?\n\nPatient: I believe it. Even when the chart shows no bad outcome, I think a delay or something I said the wrong way must have harmed someone somehow, and that eventually everyone will find out.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone you trust been able to convince you, even briefly, that you may not have harmed someone?\n\nPatient: Not really. People can tell me the chart looks fine, but I still think something may have been missed—that a delay or poorly worded message harmed someone, and it’ll come out eventually.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are signs right now that other people already know you harmed someone, even if they have not said it directly?\n\nPatient: Yes. I notice people being brief with me or looking at me a certain way, and I think they may already know something happened but aren’t saying it. Even though I can’t point to anything definite in the chart, it feels like they’ll all find out eventually.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your coworkers are talking about your having harmed someone, even when you cannot hear what they are saying?\n\nPatient: I can’t hear them talking, but I do worry they know. If they’re being brief or acting differently, I think it may be because they’ve figured out that I harmed someone somehow, even though I can’t find anything in the chart.\n\nDoctor: If you were shown clear proof that no patient was harmed, would you still believe you had harmed someone?\n\nPatient: I think I still would. I’d see the proof, but I’d keep thinking maybe there was some delay or message from me that caused harm quietly and just hasn’t been found yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear that you harmed someone could be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know the chart doesn’t show a bad outcome, but it’s hard for me to believe the fear is mistaken. I keep thinking a routine delay or an imperfect message from me may have quietly harmed someone, and eventually people will find out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this belief that you harmed someone comes from your own mind, or does it ever feel like it is being put into your mind by some outside force?\n\nPatient: It feels like it comes from my own mind. I don’t feel like anyone or anything is putting the thought there—it just keeps coming back, and I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: When this fear comes up, do you do anything specific to try to undo it or make sure no harm happened?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I do anything specific to undo it. I mostly just keep going over it in my head, wondering if I missed something.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is actual evidence that you harmed a patient?\n\nPatient: When I review the chart, there isn’t evidence that anything bad happened. But I still feel like a delay or something I said imperfectly may have caused harm that just hasn’t been recognized yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that this belief might be unreasonable or not make sense?\n\nPatient: I know the chart doesn’t show a bad outcome, so part of me can see why it might not make sense. But I still believe maybe a routine delay or something I communicated poorly caused harm that hasn’t been noticed yet. I keep thinking eventually everyone will find out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could be mistaken about your coworkers’ briefness or looks meaning they know you harmed someone?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe I could be, but it doesn’t feel that way. Even with nothing in the chart showing a bad outcome, I keep thinking a routine delay or something I said wrong may have harmed someone, and that eventually everyone will realize it.\n\nDoctor: Outside of this fear about having harmed a patient, do you worry excessively about many other parts of your life?\n\nPatient: Not really. It’s mostly this one fear that keeps coming back.\n\nDoctor: When this fear comes up, does it feel senseless or unreasonable to you?\n\nPatient: I can see how it sounds unreasonable when I look at the chart and there’s no evidence anything went wrong. But it doesn’t feel senseless to me. I keep thinking some small delay or something I communicated imperfectly may have quietly harmed a patient, and that eventually people will find out.\n\nDoctor: When this fear comes up, do you find yourself mentally reviewing the event over and over to try to be sure no one was harmed?\n\nPatient: I'm not sure. I do think about it a lot, but I don't know that I'm deliberately reviewing it to reassure myself.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

Even if I review the chart and there’s no sign anything went wrong, I still believe some delay or imperfect message from me must have harmed someone somehow. Even when the chart shows no bad outcome, I think a delay or something I said the wrong way must have harmed someone somehow, and that eventually everyone will find out.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone you trust been able to convince you, even briefly, that you may not have harmed someone?\n\nPatient: Not really. People can tell me the chart looks fine, but I still think something may have been missed—that a delay or poorly worded message harmed someone, and it’ll come out eventually.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are signs right now that other people already know you harmed someone, even if they have not said it directly?\n\nPatient: Yes. I notice people being brief with me or looking at me a certain way, and I think they may already know something happened but aren’t saying it. Even though I can’t point to anything definite in the chart, it feels like they’ll all find out eventually.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your coworkers are talking about your having harmed someone, even when you cannot hear what they are saying?\n\nPatient: I can’t hear them talking, but I do worry they know. If they’re being brief or acting differently, I think it may be because they’ve figured out that I harmed someone somehow, even though I can’t find anything in the chart.\n\nDoctor: If you were shown clear proof that no patient was harmed, would you still believe you had harmed someone?\n\nPatient: I think I still would. I’d see the proof, but I’d keep thinking maybe there was some delay or message from me that caused harm quietly and just hasn’t been found yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear that you harmed someone could be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know the chart doesn’t show a bad outcome, but it’s hard for me to believe the fear is mistaken. I keep thinking a routine delay or an imperfect message from me may have quietly harmed someone, and eventually people will find out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this belief that you harmed someone comes from your own mind, or does it ever feel like it is being put into your mind by some outside force?\n\nPatient: It feels like it comes from my own mind. Even when the chart shows no bad outcome, I think a delay or something I said the wrong way must have harmed someone somehow, and that eventually everyone will find out.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone you trust been able to convince you, even briefly, that you may not have harmed someone?\n\nPatient: Not really. People can tell me the chart looks fine, but I still think something may have been missed—that a delay or poorly worded message harmed someone, and it’ll come out eventually.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are signs right now that other people already know you harmed someone, even if they have not said it directly?\n\nPatient: Yes. I notice people being brief with me or looking at me a certain way, and I think they may already know something happened but aren’t saying it. Even though I can’t point to anything definite in the chart, it feels like they’ll all find out eventually.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your coworkers are talking about your having harmed someone, even when you cannot hear what they are saying?\n\nPatient: I can’t hear them talking, but I do worry they know. If they’re being brief or acting differently, I think it may be because they’ve figured out that I harmed someone somehow, even though I can’t find anything in the chart.\n\nDoctor: If you were shown clear proof that no patient was harmed, would you still believe you had harmed someone?\n\nPatient: I think I still would. I’d see the proof, but I’d keep thinking maybe there was some delay or message from me that caused harm quietly and just hasn’t been found yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear that you harmed someone could be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know the chart doesn’t show a bad outcome, but it’s hard for me to believe the fear is mistaken. I keep thinking a routine delay or an imperfect message from me may have quietly harmed someone, and eventually people will find out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this belief that you harmed someone comes from your own mind, or does it ever feel like it is being put into your mind by some outside force?\n\nPatient: It feels like it comes from my own mind. I don’t feel like anyone or anything is putting the thought there—it just keeps coming back, and I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: When this fear comes up, do you do anything specific to try to undo it or make sure no harm happened?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I do anything specific to undo it. I mostly just keep going over it in my head, wondering if I missed something.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is actual evidence that you harmed a patient?\n\nPatient: When I review the chart, there isn’t evidence that anything bad happened. But I still feel like a delay or something I said imperfectly may have caused harm that just hasn’t been recognized yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that this belief might be unreasonable or not make sense?\n\nPatient: I know the chart doesn’t show a bad outcome, so part of me can see why it might not make sense. But I still believe maybe a routine delay or something I communicated poorly caused harm that hasn’t been noticed yet. I keep thinking eventually everyone will find out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could be mistaken about your coworkers’ briefness or looks meaning they know you harmed someone?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe I could be, but it doesn’t feel that way. Even with nothing in the chart showing a bad outcome, I keep thinking a routine delay or something I said wrong may have harmed someone, and that eventually everyone will realize it.\n\nDoctor: Outside of this fear about having harmed a patient, do you worry excessively about many other parts of your life?\n\nPatient: Not really. It’s mostly this one fear that keeps coming back.\n\nDoctor: When this fear comes up, does it feel senseless or unreasonable to you?\n\nPatient: I can see how it sounds unreasonable when I look at the chart and there’s no evidence anything went wrong. But it doesn’t feel senseless to me. I keep thinking some small delay or something I communicated imperfectly may have quietly harmed a patient, and that eventually people will find out.\n\nDoctor: When this fear comes up, do you find yourself mentally reviewing the event over and over to try to be sure no one was harmed?\n\nPatient: I'm not sure. I do think about it a lot, but I don't know that I'm deliberately reviewing it to reassure myself.\n\nDoctor: Right now, is it possible in your mind that you did not harm any patient?\n\nPatient: I mean, I can see on the chart that there’s no bad outcome, but I still don’t really believe I didn’t cause harm. It feels like something small I did—an ordinary delay or a message that wasn’t clear enough—hurt someone quietly, and eventually everyone will find out.\n\nDoctor: When this thought that you harmed a patient comes up, do you experience it as unwanted and try to resist it?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really try to resist it. I look at the chart and know there’s nothing showing a bad outcome, but I still believe some routine delay or unclear message from me may have harmed someone quietly. It feels like people will eventually find out.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you are, as a person, dangerous?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I am. Not that I want to hurt anyone, but I keep thinking the small mistakes or imperfections I make clinically mean I don’t deserve people’s trust. Like they prove I’m a dangerous doctor."
